=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a sequence of integers (A_1,\dots,A_N) of length N. This sequence satisfies 0\le A_i < i for each i=1,\dots,N.
Find the number of permutations (P_1,\dots,P_N) of (1,\dots,N) that satisfy the following conditions, modulo 998244353.

- For each i=1,\dots,N:
- P_j > P_i for any integer j with A_i < j < i 
- P_{A_i} < P_i if A_i > 0



For the sequence (A_1,\dots,A_N) given in the input, it is guaranteed that there exists a permutation satisfying the conditions.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N

Output

Print the number of permutations satisfying the conditions, modulo 998244353.

Constraints


- 1\le N\le 3\times 10^5
- 0\le A_i \lt i
- For A_1,\dots,A_N, there exists a permutation satisfying the conditions in the problem statement.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

4
0 1 0 3

Sample Output 1

3

There are three such permutations: (2, 3, 1, 4), (2, 4, 1, 3), and (3, 4, 1, 2).

Sample Input 2

22
0 1 2 2 2 2 2 2 1 9 9 9 9 0 14 15 15 15 14 19 19 19

Sample Output 2

353820794

The answer is 353820794, which is 2350309500 modulo 998244353.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

- If k=0: x_i < x_{s_1} (if m>0), no lower bound.

- If k=m: x_{s_m} < x_i (no upper bound).

Thus each new value is inserted into one of the gaps between current stack values, or below the bottom, or above the top. It must be distinct and all values 1..N used. The final Cartesian tree of P has parent relationships determined by stack operations: A_i is the node after popping; i becomes right child of A_i if A_i>0, else root; the popped nodes (above A_i) become left subtree of i? Let's recall insertion algorithm for min-Cartesian tree:
To insert new node i with value x_i:
 last = 0
 while stack not empty and value[stack[-1]] > x_i:
   last = stack.pop()
 if stack not empty:
   right[stack[-1]] = i
 left[i] = last
 stack.append(i)
This is exactly. So A_i = stack[-1] after popping if any; if A_i=0, i becomes root. Popped last becomes left child of i. Thus the final Cartesian tree is determined by A_i? Let's detail algorithm:

Input N, list A length N (1-indexed perhaps).

We need build tree arrays left = [0]*(N+1), right = [0]*(N+1). root = 0.
stack = [] list of indices.

For i from 1 to N:
    ai = A[i-1]
    last = 0
    if ai == 0:
        while stack:
            last = stack.pop()
        # after popping all, last is bottom of stack (old root)
        left[i] = last
        root = i
    else:
        # ai must be in stack. Pop until top == ai.
        # But if ai not in stack, invalid; guaranteed.
        while stack and stack[-1] != ai:
            last = stack.pop()
        # Now stack[-1] == ai (should)
        # If ai is not top? after loop top is ai. If ai not in stack, stack empty; invalid. We can assume.
        # last is the last popped element (immediately above ai), or 0 if none.
        left[i] = last
        right[ai] = i
        # stack still contains ai and below; do not pop ai
    stack.append(i)

Need be careful: If ai != 0 and stack[-1] == ai initially, while not executed, last=0, left[i]=0, right[ai]=i. This means i becomes right child of ai with no left child. Good.

We can compute subtree sizes by iterative DFS:
order = []
stack = [root]
while stack:
    v = stack.pop()
    order.append(v)
    if left[v]: stack.append(left[v])
    if right[v]: stack.append(right[v])
Then process reversed(order):
    sz[v] = 1
    if left[v]: sz[v] += sz[left[v]]
    if right[v]: sz[v] += sz[right[v]]
This is O(N). Need ensure tree connected; valid.

Let's write code:

import sys
MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    left = [0]*(N+1)
    right = [0]*(N+1)
    st = []
    root = 0
    fact = 1
    for i, ai in enumerate(A, 1):
        fact = fact * i % MOD
        last = 0
        if ai == 0:
            while st:
                last = st.pop()
            left[i] = last
            root = i
        else:
            # pop until ai is top
            while st and st[-1] != ai:
                last = st.pop()
            # now st[-1] should be ai
            # If invalid, maybe handle:
            # if not st or st[-1] != ai: ...
            left[i] = last
            right[ai] = i
        st.append(i)

    # compute subtree sizes
    sz = [0]*(N+1)
    order = []
    st = [root]
    while st:
        v = st.pop()
        order.append(v)
        l = left[v]
        if l:
            st.append(l)
        r = right[v]
        if r:
            st.append(r)
    for v in reversed(order):
        s = 1
        l = left[v]
        if l:
            s += sz[l]
        r = right[v]
        if r:
            s += sz[r]
        sz[v] = s

    prod = 1
    for v in range(1, N+1):
        prod = prod * sz[v] % MOD

    ans = fact * pow(prod, MOD-2, MOD) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's prove hook-length formula for binary tree with left/right children:
f(v) = C(size(v)-1, size(left)) * f(left) * f(right) if both; if one child, C(n-1, size_child). This yields f(v) = (n-1)! / ∏_{u in subtree(v), u≠v} size(u)? Let's derive:
Assume f(child)=child_size! / ∏_{u in child} size(u).
Then f(v)= (n-1)!/(L! R!) * L!/prod_L * R!/prod_R = (n-1)!/(prod_L prod_R).
Need equal n!/(n * prod_L prod_R) = (n-1)!/prod_L prod_R. Yes. So product includes size(v)=n. Good.

Yes, in preorder, parent is visited before any descendant. Therefore in reversed preorder, parent appears after all descendants. Let's write final code:

import sys
MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    left = [0]*(N+1)
    right = [0]*(N+1)
    st = []
    root = 0
    fact = 1
    for i in range(1, N+1):
        fact = fact * i % MOD
        ai = data[i]
        last = 0
        if ai == 0:
            while st:
                last = st.pop()
            left[i] = last
            root = i
        else:
            while st and st[-1] != ai:
                last = st.pop()
            # In a valid input, st[-1] == ai here.
            left[i] = last
            right[ai] = i
        st.append(i)
    del data

    sz = [0]*(N+1)
    order = []
    st = [root]
    while st:
        v = st.pop()
        order.append(v)
        l = left[v]
        if l:
            st.append(l)
        r = right[v]
        if r:
            st.append(r)

    prod = 1
    for v in reversed(order):
        s = 1
        l = left[v]
        if l:
            s += sz[l]
        r = right[v]
        if r:
            s += sz[r]
        sz[v] = s
        prod = prod * s % MOD

    ans = fact * pow(prod, MOD-2, MOD) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's think about if the original conditions require P_j > P_i for j in (A_i,i), which includes nodes that are not descendants of i? We argued they are left subtree descendants. Let's prove for a node i, its left subtree consists exactly of nodes with indices between A_i and i. In the tree shape from A_i, at insertion i, left[i] is the old right child of A_i, which is the root of the subtree containing all nodes in the interval (A_i, i) that were in A_i's right subtree. Future insertions with index > i cannot enter this interval, and future insertions with index < i cannot happen. So left subtree of i is fixed to that interval. Thus heap property P_i < all left subtree values. Good.

Now, let's think about if the heap labeling count might allow P_i < left subtree but not necessarily P_j > P_i for all j in (A_i,i) if some j in that interval is not in left subtree? We proved all are in left subtree. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "P_{A_i} < P_i if A_i > 0" for nodes where A_i is not parent but ancestor. Heap property along path ensures. But does our tree shape guarantee A_i is an ancestor? We argued yes. Let's prove by induction: At insertion i, A_i is parent. Later, if A_i is popped by some future node k, A_i becomes left child of k, so remains ancestor of its entire subtree including i. If A_i is not popped, it remains ancestor. Thus yes. Good.
